#2818. The troubling Nets trend holding them back for yet another year

Jordi Fernández sat inside the visitors news conference room at Madison Square Garden and looked at the paper boxscore in front of him following the Nets' 103-90 loss Monday to the reigning champs.

"I mean, you hold a team to 103 points in the NBA, you should be able to win a lot of games," Fernández said.

Even if tanking is in Brooklyn's rearview mirror, its offense remains stuck in the past.

In Fernández's first season, Cam Thomas — who is no longer in the NBA — was the leading scorer on a 26-win team that ranked 28th in scoring (105.1) and offensive rating. Last season's 20-win squad — featuring Michael Porter Jr. and a collection of underwhelming young role players — finished last in the league in scoring (105.9) and offensive rating.

Brooklyn Nets forward Julius Randle (30) drives to the basket against New York Knicks center Andre Drummond (0) on Monday, Nov. 2, 2026, in New York. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

This season, the Nets received a much-needed infusion of talent — two-time All-NBA selection Julius Randle and No. 6 overall pick Mikel Brown Jr., along with Mo Wagner and Keon Ellis — but the team has struggled to develop offensive chemistry.

Through seven games, the Nets (1-6) rank 29th in scoring (105.6), 28th in offensive rating, 29th in assist to turnover ratio (1.21), 28th in turnover percentage (18.3) and 28th in field goal percentage (41.7).

Entering Wednesday's game against the Hornets (3-5) — which may see second-year star Kon Knueppel make his season debut — Brooklyn is averaging 94 points over its past three games. In Monday's loss to the Knicks, the Nets scored a season-low 90 points, allowing a late lead to evaporate as they shot 3-for-16 with five turnovers, while scoring 11 points in the fourth quarter.

"I think it's more about us not being organized, and then if you have empty possessions, you don't run anything for anybody, like nobody's in the right spot," Fernández said. "You don't touch the paint. You don't move. You don't reverse the ball, and you get either no shots or terrible shots. … Everybody's got to do their jobs because whether we call a play or not, you got to run to your spots, and we didn't do it enough times, and that's the frustrating thing. When we do it, we get good results. When we don't do it, we get bad results.

Brooklyn Nets head coach Jordi Fernandez watches his team during the first half of an NBA basketball game against the New York Knicks, Monday, Nov. 2, 2026. AP Photo/Frank Franklin II

"We still gotta run more and find those easy ones. We're not there yet, but I think we're gonna keep working, keep watching it, and then we'll see good results. … The things that we didn't do as well in the fourth, I'm going to take responsibility and help them."

Randle is averaging a career-high 4.1 turnovers per game and shooting 41.5 percent from the field, well down from his career mark (47.2). Brown is shooting under 30 percent from the field in the first five games of his career. And the point guard spot has been split by a pair of 20-year-olds (Egor Dëmin, Nolan Traore).

Mikel Brown Jr. (0) of the Brooklyn Nets drives to the basket against the New York Knicks on Monday, Nov. 2, 2026, at Madison Square Garden in New York. NBAE via Getty Images

There has been bad spacing, poor ball movement and constant confusion. There is plenty of blame to go around.

"Just gotta remain disciplined," Randle said. "We know what we need to do … you can't just put it on one person. It's a team game. It's five players out there, so we got to be better at communicating, understanding the moment, what's going on. It's all five of us. We all got to be better out there."

#2817. Napoli make approach over loan-to-buy move for Barcelona fan-favourite midfielder – report

According to Calciomercato (h/t SPORT), Napoli have set sights on Barcelona midfielder Gavi, with sporting director Giovanni Manna exploring the possibility of a loan move in January.

The Serie A giants have been keeping a close eye on the La Masia graduate's situation at Barcelona, given that he has struggled for regular minutes under Hansi Flick.

As such, the report states that Napoli have explored the possibility of signing Gavi in the upcoming winter transfer window.

The Partenopei would ideally prefer a loan deal with an option to buy for the Spanish midfielder as it would allow them to pursue the player without committing to a significant initial transfer fee.

Manna has approached Gavi's entourage to understand his position and assess whether there could be an opportunity to negotiate a deal.

Gavi has struggled for minutes. (Photo by David Ramos/Getty Images)

The Italian giants' interest emanates due to the midfielder's current lack of regular playing opportunities this season under Flick.

After all, the 22-year-old fan-favourite midfielder has only played 211 minutes across eight games for Barcelona this season, starting only twice.

Gavi, as things stand, is behind the likes of Pedri, Rodri, Fermin Lopez, Dani Olmo, and even Marc Bernal in the pecking order at the Catalan club.

Despite his situation being far from ideal, Gavi remains fully committed to Barcelona and has no intention of pursuing a move away from the club.

Indeed, the La Masia product understands that patience is needed in his current circumstance even though he is not entirely pleased with the amount of minutes he has played so far in 2026/27.

Fully committed to Barcelona. (Photo by Alex Caparros/Getty Images)

Several Premier League clubs expressed interest in his situation last summer, but the midfielder made it clear that he had no desire to leave Barcelona. And his position has not changed since then despite his reduced prominence in the team.

The Blaugrana, for their part, also value their young midfielder immensely and have zero intention of letting him go anytime soon.

Even though Flick has not used him regularly, the German tactician rates the World Cup winner very highly and sees him as a key member of the squad.

As such, Napoli's approach to lure Gavi away from Barcelona on a potential loan deal with a buy option is unlikely to lead to anything substantial.

#2813. Kawhi Leonard injury update: Raptors star finishes knee treatment but still out

Kawhi Leonard is headed back to Toronto. Getting back on the court is the next step.

The Raptors announced Tuesday, Nov. 3 that Leonard, 35, has completed treatment for swelling in his left knee and will begin the return-to-play process in Toronto. The team will update his progress in one week.

Leonard has missed five consecutive games. He felt something in the knee during pregame warmups Oct. 25 and was scratched from a loss to the Minnesota Timberwolves. An MRI showed no structural damage.

Coach Darko Rajakovic said Oct. 29 that Leonard was getting his treatment outside of Toronto. At the time, NBA insider Chris Haynes reported Leonard would miss at least three to four weeks. The Raptors didn't say Tuesday whether that window has changed.

Leonard already has injury history with the right knee. Leonard tore the ACL in his right knee in the 2021 NBA playoffs and the meniscus in the 2023 playoffs – both with the Los Angeles Clippers.

Toronto brought the two-time NBA Finals MVP back this offseason, sending Brandon Ingram, Gradey Dick and draft picks to Los Angeles. The deal was agreed to in late June, then sat until September while the NBA investigated the Clippers and Leonard for a salary cap circumvention scheme. The league fined Leonard $700,000. He signed a two-year, $115 million extension with Toronto soon after.

Leonard played in the Raptors' first two games, both wins. He averaged 19.5 points, 7.5 rebounds and 3.5 steals but shot just 32.4% from the field. Last season, he played 65 games for the Clippers and averaged 27.9 points.

The Raptors (4-3) are 2-3 without him. The next update is due Nov. 10.

BOLOGNA, ITALY – OCTOBER 17: Francesco Pio Esposito of FC Internazionale Milano has a shot saved by Lukasz Skorupski of Bologna FC 1909 during the Serie A match between Bologna FC and FC Internazionale at Stadio Renato Dall'Ara on October 17, 2026 in Bologna, Italy. #2807. Jets sign OL Blake Freeland off Bengals' practice squad

The Jets signed offensive tackle Blake Freeland off the Bengals’ practice squad, the team announced Tuesday.

In a corresponding move, the Jets placed offensive lineman Max Mitchell on injured reserve. They also released cornerback Tre Brown from injured reserve.

Freeland joined the Bengals’ practice squad after the Colts waived him on Aug. 31. In the preseason, he played 144 offensive snaps over the Colts’ three games.

Freeland, who played collegiately at BYU, was selected by Indianapolis in the fourth round of the 2023 draft. He made nine starts as a rookie, while appearing in 15 total games. In 2024, he appeared in eight games and logged 36 special teams snaps and 23 on offense. Freeland missed the entire 2025 season with a fracture in his right leg.

Mitchell was diagnosed with a concussion during a Week 7 practice, and he has missed the past two games. This season, he has played 22 special teams snaps over the first six weeks. Mitchell, who played collegiately at Louisiana, was selected by the Jets in the fourth round of the 2022 draft and has played 51 games.

Brown went on injured reserve on Aug. 30.

#2803. NFL Secret Superstars: 3 under-the-radar AFC standouts in Week 8

Superstars may lead the discussion when it comes to success in the NFL, but more often than not, it's the guys you barely know who will make the plays needed when they're needed. Such players can be underrated for all kinds of reasons. Perhaps they're playing in a smaller market, and the media isn't appropriately trumpeting their exploits. Maybe they're hidden in a depth chart behind bigger names who may not be doing as much. Or, it's always possible that a wayward coach hasn't quite figured out that one guy needs elevation to bring better results to the team.

Whatever the reason, we at SB Nation always like to bring the underrated names to you with the offseason Hidden Gems and in-season Secret Superstars series. You can also catch up on the AFC and NFC Secret Superstars for the 2026 season right here.

Here are our AFC Secret Superstars for Week 8 of the 2026 regular season. And if you haven't yet, download the new SB Nation app, so you can better read our stuff and join the conversation wherever you are!

(All advanced metrics courtesy of Next Gen Stats, Pro Football Focus, Sports Info Solutions, and Sumer Sports unless otherwise indicated).

The Kansas City Chiefs went for a complete overhaul of their run game this past offseason. They brought back offensive coordinator Eric Bieniemy to do the job he'd done from 2018 to 2022, they signed Kenneth Walker III to a three-year, $43.05 million contract with $28.7 million guaranteed, and they selected Nebraska running back Emmett Johnson with the 151st overall pick in the fifth round of the 2026 draft.

Of those moves, the Johnson pick made the fewest waves, but if you had studied Johnson's college tape, there might have been a bit of a zing there. In 2025, the 5'10", 202-pound Johnson ran the ball 251 times for 1,450 yards, 12 touchdowns, 68 forced missed tackles, and 36 runs of 10 or more yards.

Johnson did okay at the combine, but not in ways that represented his on-field dynamism, which may be one reason he dropped as far as he did. We're assuming that the Chiefs are quite happy that it worked out the way it did, especially on Sunday against the Denver Broncos. Kansas City lost that game 28-24 despite a last-second drive that ended on the Denver 12-yard line with a Malcolm Roach sack of Patrick Mahomes, but Johnson went off in that game. He grabbed five catches on five targets for 128 yards and a touchdown, and his one rushing attempt for 15 yards saw him break at least five tackles.

"Obviously Kenneth is the guy, but you want to be able to take carries away from him, so we can have him for the full entire season," Mahomes said postgame. "If Emmett can come in and be a change of pace, break tackles and make plays, catch the ball, do all that different type of stuff, defenses can't relax. I thought he did a great job today, and he's done great the entire time he's been here. As the season goes on, we'll continue to utilize him more and more, but at the same time utilizing Kenneth."

So, the run game is all set. The Chiefs desperately need receiver help before the trade deadline, but at least they have the ground attack going for them, which is nice.

Speaking of surprise backs who are showing up all over the place, there's Braelon Allen of the New York Jets. The 6′ 1¼", 235-pound Allen impressed at Wisconsin as a big power back with some wiggle and explosiveness — he had 592 carries for 3,482 yards, 25 touchdowns, 143 forced missed tackles, and 77 runs of 10 or more yards for the Badgers from 2021-2023 — but that still had him lasting until the 134th overall pick in the fourth round of the 2024 draft, which is where the Jets got him.

With Breece Hall as the main man in the Jets' backfield, it took Allen a while to get his share of the carries. He had just 110 rushing attempts combined in the 2024 and 2025 seasons, but with Hall much more of an injury concern this season (he's missed five straight games with a quadriceps injury), Allen has gotten his shot, and he's made the most of it — especially recently. His 21-carry, 147-yard, one-touchdown performance against the Las Vegas Raiders last Sunday in a frustrating 38-33 loss was just the latest in a series of great games.

Allen also has 20 forced missed tackles since Week 5 (only Jahmyr Gibbs of the Detroit Lions has more with 26), and nobody in the NFL has more runs of 10 or more yards in that timeframe than Allen's 18.

"Guys up front moving people, just being patient and just letting the game come to me," Allen said, when asked postgame about the secret to his recent success. "Not trying to force anything. Just going by my reads and progressing right. Like I said, the guys up front, they make it happen."

So, how did it feel to score on your first six drives and still lose the game?

"The objective every week is to win," Allen said. "When you don't get that done it's hard to—obviously there's positives always, and I love our progress as a team and as an offense. There are takeaways for sure, positive takeaways, but like I said, the goal is to win. When you don't do that it's hard to enjoy."

Perhaps it would have helped if Allen had more than three carries (for 26 yards) in the second half? When your defense is busy blowing a 23-14 halftime lead, and you're allowing Kirk Cousins to look like Patrick Mahomes in the second half, perhaps toting the rock and bleeding the clock are better options. The Jets had a 19:17-10:43 time of possession advantage in the first half, but at the end of the game, the Raiders had marched back to make it about as even as possible — 30:02 to 29:58 in Gang Green's favor.

Maybe the coaching will catch up to Allen's exploits, and the 2-6 Jets can start to get in front of those games. In the meantime, it helps that Braelon Allen has arrived.

In August, Titans edge-rusher Jacob Martin was asked about the ideal mentality of the his new defensive line after he signed a two-year, $9 million contract with $4.5 million guaranteed.

"We go in there and f**k s**t up," Martin said. "And the linebackers clean it up. I am super thankful to be here, thankful for the opportunity. The team is great, guys are great. A lot of young talent in this building, and I am excited to be a part of this organization, excited to grow, excited to build with these guys. The sky is the limit for sure."

Martin obviously said all of that before the Titans traded Jeffery Simmons, the NFL's best interior defensive lineman, to the Chicago Bears last week for a bevy of draft picks. It's less encouraging when your new team is in full-on fire-sale mode, but against the Cincinnati Bengals on Sunday, Martin was bound and determined to make up for Simmons' absence by any means necessary. Martin has been a good player throughout his career in fits and starts. but you don't play for eight teams in nine seasons if you're one of Those Guys.

He was on Sunday, though. Martin had three sacks, five quarterback hits, and two quarterback hurries in Tennessee's 43-36 overtime loss, and while Joe Burrow absolutely went off for 462 passing yards and six total touchdowns (five passing and one rushing), safety Amani Hooker intercepted three of Burrow's passes, and Martin harassed Burrow and left tackle Orlando Brown Jr. ceaselessly. Inside moves, outside the arc, bull-rushes — the Bengals could do nothing to stop him.

It's important for the rest of the Titans' defensive line to follow suit and "f**k s**t up" as Martin noted. Certainly, with seven sacks and 29 pressures this season, he's doing his part.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2802. Sturgis girls cross country qualifies for state finals for fourth consecutive season

The St. Joseph County area will be represented at the MHSAA state cross country finals this weekend.

The Sturgis girls are headed to the state finals for the fourth consecutive year. A pair of boys' teams will also run at the finals, with Centreville and White Pigeon both qualifying. Several individuals were not on qualifying teams but ran their way to a state finals appearance.

The Sturgis girls finished third overall at the regional race held at Portage West Middle School. Portage Central was the host of the regional here, where the Trojans scored 118 points. Otsego won the regional with 29 points, and Plainwell also qualified for the girls with 100 points as the Wolverine Conference teams swept the state-qualifying spots.

Alice Starkey paced the orange and black on the day. She finished 10th overall at the regional round and ran a personal-best time of 19:33 for the day. Lulu Park was 18th overall at 20:12, while Leilani Machuca was 26th at 20:37. Emmy Farber ran to 31st at 20:56; Ally Farber was 33rd with a time of 21:01, followed by Annmarie Taylor in 37th at 21:11. Eleanor Park came in 43rd at 21:36.

COLON ADVANCES: Colon finds itself back in regional final after playoff-opening win

SATURDAY FOOTBALL RECAP: Mendon, Marcellus and Constantine advance in playoffs with Saturday wins

Also qualifying for the state finals in Division 2 was David Kore of Three Rivers. He had an impressive day on the course with a fifth-place finishing time of 16:10.

The Centreville boys are headed to the state finals as a team. The Bulldogs qualified by finishing third at the regional round with 82 points. Hanover-Horton was first as a team; it posted 48 points, while Jonesville also qualified for the finals by finishing second with 54. The regional was hosted at Ella Sharp Park in Jackson.

The Centreville boys were led by Will Hulin yet again. He placed fourth as an individual with a time of 15:53, finishing just a handful of seconds behind the winner, who was Cooper Flick of Hanover-Horton (15:44).

Centreville also had three additional runners inside the Top 20 for the day. Max Reed ran to 10th place overall at 16:23, while Nick Overweg came in 15th at 16:26 and Will Swanwick was 19th at 16:47. Corban Brueck posted 18:14 for 43rd, Landon Weis was 49th at 18:29 and Tanner Bingaman came in 50th overall at 18:37.

Constantine will be represented by a trio of runners at the finals. Aaron Hofmeister finished 12th overall for the boys with a time of 17:31. The Constantine girls had two qualifiers: Allison Burns ran a time of 20:54 that was good for eighth place overall, while Anna Davidhizar came in 11th place at 21:20.

Bronson will also be sending a representative to the state finals. For the girls, Alonna Goodsell punched her ticket to the finals with a 13th-place finish with a time of 20:33 on the day.

The White Pigeon boys are headed to the state finals. The Chiefs ran to third place as a team at the regional hosted at Portage West Middle School with 67 points. Kalamazoo Hackett won the regional with 25 points as a team; Bridgman came in second as a team with 58.

The Chiefs finished with four runners inside the Top 20 individual spots. They were led by Aiden Jackson, who came in 12th overall at 18:26. Zack Wiley finished 14th with a time of 18:50 while William Throw was 16th (18:55) and Isaac Doubblestein finished 19th (19:28). Matthew McGahan (23rd, 19:42), Corey White (28th, 20:08) and Tanner Ritchie (39th, 21:12) also ran for the Chiefs on the day.

For the girls, Aubrey Miller is headed to state as an individual. She finished 18th at the regional round with a time of 23:11.

Colon's Belle Noga is also headed to the state finals as an individual. She placed 16th overall at the regional round, running a time of 22:42.

The MHSAA state cross country finals will be run on Saturday, Nov. 7. Michigan International Speedway is once again the host site for the finals for the Lower Peninsula.

Races will take place throughout the day. The Division 4 boys get things started with a 9:30 a.m. race start. The day will conclude with the Division 1 girls running at approximately 3:30 p.m., and there will be a pair of awards presentations — Divisions 3 and 4 will be at 12:15 p.m. while Divisions 1 and 2 are scheduled to have awards ceremonies at 4 p.m.

#2795. Cowboys news: Jerry Jones likes the direction of his defense

Before halftime, the Dallas Cowboys led the Arizona Cardinals 14-3, and Christian Parker's defense played lights-out. Something changed in the second half, and the defense gave up four straight touchdown drives to end regulation, and the Cowboys lost in overtime.

Through it all, Jerry Jones said he still believes in his defense and sees the unit trending in the right direction.

The Cowboys owner and general manager still believes in Christian Parker.

"We've been changing," Jones said. "We've changed out on our defense, we've changed out our coaching staff, complete coaching staff. We've changed out the majority of the players, really. And in today's building of a team, you do it with a combination of draft, which draft picks have to have a little seasoning most of the time."

"And therein lies the inconsistency that we're seeing, even within a game where we played so well in the first half and came back and didn't play as well in the second half. That is a disappointment, but not a surprise… It does not call for massive, or not massive, it does not call for very succinct, important changes because I like the way we're trending."

Will the Cowboys' continued disappointment cause a shake-up in the coaching staff at the end of the season?

Jerry will not fire Schottenheimer during this season, but after it feels plausible. Schottenheimer is in the second year of a four-year contract, and while Jerry hates the idea of paying a coach to not coach, he will not want another year of what he's currently watching. The problem remains the same as it has been for years. The defense under first-year, first-time defensive coordinator Christian Parker continues to play at a level that is no longer bewildering but a state that will get people fired, most notably himself.

Brandon Aubrey hasn't been the same clutch long-distance kicker this season.

Although Aubrey's kickoffs are undeniably the best in the league, which give Dallas better field position than any other team in the NFL, his field goals have been an issue. On Sunday against the Arizona Cardinals, he had makes from 31, 35 and 41, and he made all three extra points.

The Cowboys would net some quality draft capital if they moved on from George Pickens.

Pickens is playing on the one-year franchise tag ($27.3 million), and a second tag next offseason would cost Dallas about $32.8 million. That's a problem. Cornerback Joey Porter Jr. is also playing without an extension and wants upward of $30 million per year — and the Cowboys only get one tag.

An extension won't be cheap either. Solak estimates Pickens would command north of $33 million per year, worryingly close to the $34 million CeeDee Lamb makes.

It's the year 2028, and Jason Witten is being introduced as the next head coach of the Dallas Cowboys.

Former Cowboys tight end Jason Witten was named the offensive coordinator and play caller for the remainder of the 2026 season by the University of Oklahoma. Witten was hired as the Sooners' tight ends coach at the start of the year for his first collegiate coaching job, and now takes over the offense after Oklahoma's decision to dismiss offensive coordinator Ben Arbuckle.

#2794. NBA rescinds Luka Dončić's technical foul during win over Heat, bringing him back to 1 on the season

Luka Dončić's latest technical foul has been called off.

The league rescinded a technical foul the Los Angeles Lakers star picked up during LA's 138-117 win over the Miami Heat on Monday night. The foul, which Dončić earned in the second quarter at Crypto.com Arena, was reviewed by the league Tuesday.

That has brought Dončić to a single technical foul in eight games this season.

The incident Monday happened after Dončić reacted to a call an official made, which led to a candid conversation between Dončić and the officials on the broadcast.

"I clapped. That's a technical? Come on now," Dončić told the official. "If it was anybody else, you know it's not a technical. Come on now."

Klay Thompson hit the free throw after the technical, which brought the Heat within five points at the time.

Dončić was asked about the technical after the game, but he declined to get into it.

Dončić finished with 42 points, 13 assists and 7 rebounds in the win. He's averaged 43.4 points per game over the last five outings, which included a 52-point night against the Utah Jazz on Oct. 25.

Dončić has had issues with technical fouls. He was suspended for a game last season after reaching the 16-technical foul threshold, which triggers an automatic one-game suspension. He had three straight seasons with 15 technical fouls each, and 13 during the 2024-25 campaign.

Two players in the league already have three technical fouls this season. Golden State star Draymond Green is one of them after he was ejected Monday night for an incident during the Warriors' win over the Phoenix Suns. Houston's Jabari Smith Jr. also has three.

It's still very early in the regular season, and Dončić has a long way to go before having to worry about such a penalty again. But having Monday's rescinded undoubtedly gives him extra room to work with down the stretch.

#2793. Owono ‘deeply affected’ by De Miguel’s death, says Andorra team-mate

Jesus Owono's team-mate has insisted the Andorra goalkeeper would never intend to hurt a fellow player following the death of Jesus de Miguel.

The Tenerife forward died on Tuesday morning following a collision with Owono during a Spanish second division match on Monday.

Owono was sent off for the clash having raced from his goal and struck 30-year-old De Miguel at force, with the Tenerife player subsequently suffering a cardiac arrest.

There has been shock and grief across football following the tragic incident, while Spanish police have opened an investigation into possible involuntary manslaughter.

That is, though, standard practice under Spanish law and Santa Cruz de Tenerife chief prosecutor Jose Luis Sanchez-Jauregui told Spanish news agency EFE that criminal proceedings were "very unlikely".

Andorra's Argentinian defender Lautaro Spatz took to Instagram to pay tribute to De Miguel but also to defend Owono, who has deleted his account on the social media platform.

Spatz called De Miguel's death "a tragedy that has left us completely broken, without words and with a sadness difficult to explain".

The 25-year-old continued: "I also want to have some words for my team-mate, goalkeeper and friend, who is deeply affected by what happened.

"Those of us who know him know the type of person he is, how much he is suffering in these moments and that he would never intend to hurt anyone.

"In football, unfortunate incidents happen but no-one could imagine that one of these could end in such tragic circumstances."

Fans gathered at Tenerife's Heliodoro Rodriguez Lopez stadium to lay tributes to De Miguel, with the club declaring six days of official mourning.

A memorial service will be held at the ground on Saturday evening for the striker, with supporters invited to attend.

Tuesday's domestic Spanish games were called off as a mark of respect but Champions League matches went ahead, including Barcelona's clash with Aston Villa at the Nou Camp.

A minute's silence was held ahead of the contest, which Barça won 3-0, with Raphinha scoring twice.

Speaking to CBS Sports afterwards, the Brazilian refused to talk about the game, saying: "I want to send a lot of strength to the family of Jesus. Today I'm not going to talk about football.

"For me, the truth is it was quite difficult (to play). After seeing the news, of course I didn't have good feelings. He's a fellow player and it could have happened to anyone, it could have happened to me.

"He was the same age as me, I don't know if that's why it affected me more. It's been a difficult day. Personally, it touched me a lot."

The Tenerife match was suspended after the incident, which occurred in stoppage time, with distressed players calling for immediate medical attention.

A statement from Tenerife announcing De Miguel's death read: "We stand alongside his devastated family, alongside his loved ones… that today weep for this cruel outcome."

In their own statement on Tuesday evening, Andorra said the club were "deeply shocked", adding: "The first thing we want to do is express our most sincere condolences to his family, CD Tenerife and all those who were close to him.

"We know that no words can ease such pain, but we want them to know that we are by their side and that we deeply share their sadness."

#2792. Bills reverse course on "high-maintenance" C.J. Gardner-Johnson

The Bills said they’d be keeping safety C.J. Gardner-Johnson. And now they’re not.

Tuesday’s surprise move to release the veteran defensive back directly contradicts the comments made publicly by coach Joe Brady, on the same day Gardner-Johnson received a one-game suspension for spitting on an opponent during the Week 6 game against the Raiders.

“Look, C.J.'s a member of the Buffalo Bills,” Brady said on October 19. “We brought him here for a reason, and we know that he knows that he has to play better. He knows he has to be better for this team, and, you know, just like everybody else, right, we’ve got to learn from this stuff, and we have to improve and find ways to get better as the season goes.”

As one source explained it to PFT, the Bills decided that Gardner-Johnson was simply too “high-maintenance” — and that the talent and production didn’t justify the headaches.

Plenty of other teams could have told the Bills what would happen. Gardner-Johnson has hopscotched around the league, from the Saints to the Eagles to the Lions to the Eagles (again) to the Texans to the Ravens to the Bears to the Bills.

He has been good enough to get more chances, but not good enough to keep him around in light of the issues that he brings with him.

The Bills hoped to harness his attitude in order to give the defense a lift. In the end, however, the baggage outweighed the boost.

And now Gardner-Johnson becomes a free agent again. Plenty of coaches believe they can be the one to speak to a player’s better angels. As to Gardner-Johnson, the demons have won, far too often.

#2788. Josh Jacobs needs to display more (or any) contrition

In May 2026, Packers running back Josh Jacobs crossed a line that should never be crossed. And neither he nor his representatives are acting accordingly in what should be an effort to seek redemption.

He pleaded no contest to battery of a woman. He’s fighting in court to keep the video secret. He’s responding to additional allegations through a strident statement from his lawyers attacking the accusers.

The most tone-deaf gesture of all came last Thursday night, when he celebrated a touchdown by pretending to wear, and to be released from, handcuffs.

His implausible explanation, which accused Prime Video of not showing enough of the celebration and attacked the media for interpreting the images with their lyin’ eyes, was contradicted by the full video of the event.

That’s not the way to play this. His no-contest plea is equivalent to saying, “I did it.” He should be contrite. He should be apologetic. He should be seeking redemption.

The Packers continue to stand behind him, primarily because he’s the best running back on the roster and they want to win games. Their reported interest in Colts running back Jonathan Taylor strongly suggests that, if the Packers could get Taylor, the support for Jacobs would immediately evaporate.

Here’s the basic reality. If the Packers were to cut Jacobs, would anyone sign him? The Chiefs took plenty of heat last week for adding kicker Justin Tucker to the practice squad; they may not be inclined to give another second chance to a player whose arrival would compound the negative reaction to Tucker’s arrival.

The reality, in every sport at every level, is obvious. If the player has enough skill and ability, someone will find a way to justify behavior that shouldn’t be tolerated by an industry that relies so heavily on support from the public, both directly through ticket sales and TV ratings and indirectly through taxpayer funding for stadium projects.

Still, Jacobs has done himself no favors by adopting a posture of defiance. He did something no man should ever do. He’s lucky he’s good enough at football to not lose his job over it.

If he was at the bottom of the depth chart in Green Bay, he would have been gone a long time ago.

#2786. NFL’s Secret Superstars, Week 8: 3 under-the-radar standouts from the NFC

Superstars may lead the discussion when it comes to success in the NFL, but more often than not, it's the guys you barely know who will make the plays needed when they're needed. Such players can be underrated for all kinds of reasons. Perhaps they're playing in a smaller market, and the media isn't appropriately trumpeting their exploits. Maybe they're hidden in a depth chart behind bigger names who may not be doing as much. Or, it's always possible that a wayward coach hasn't quite figured out that one guy needs elevation to bring better results to the team.

Whatever the reason, we at SB Nation always like to bring the underrated names to you with the offseason Hidden Gems and in-season Secret Superstars series. You can also catch up on the AFC and NFC Secret Superstars for the 2026 season right here.

Here are our NFC Secret Superstars for Week 8 of the 2026 regular season. And if you haven't yet, download the new SB Nation app, so you can better read our stuff and join the conversation wherever you are!

(All advanced metrics courtesy of Next Gen Stats, Pro Football Focus, Sports Info Solutions, and Sumer Sports unless otherwise indicated).

Baseball delineates its pitchers very specifically between starters and relievers. There are times when I think that NFL teams should do the same thing. If your quarterback is struggling to start a game, why should he automatically be able to finish it without question? Arizona head coach Mike LaFleur ran into this situation when his team went up against the Dallas Cowboys last Sunday. Starter Jacoby Brissett completed just 10 of 23 passes for 119 yards, no touchdowns, one interception, four sacks, and a passer rating of 41.8. When that's all you can do against a Dallas defense that should apologize for even taking the field every week, it's time to sub your starter out.

Which is what LaFleur did. Backup Gardner Minshew took his first snap of the day with 1:35 left in the third quarter with the Cardinals down 17-3, and things were never the same from there. Minshew tore through that alleged defense as it should be, completing 17 of 23 passes for 188 yards, three touchdowns, no interceptions, and a passer rating of 137.3. Everything Brissett struggled with, Minshew had on the ball right away, and that's how the Cardinals erased a major deficit to win the game in overtime, 33-30.

"Yeah, he did a good job," LaFleur said of Minshew after the win. "Obviously, extended a few plays, stuff like that, but I thought his timing was great. I mean, he's had a lot of success in this league, guys, and he's about as resilient as they come, too. If you ask Gardner Minshew, I mean, he's ready at all times. And I thought the guys made some plays for him, and he was getting the ball out of his hands. Obviously, kind of what unlocked it to start was — it starts with a six-yard completion to [receiver Devin] Duvernay, and then we obviously have that long run by TA [running back Tyler Allgeier] to unlock and loosened them up. That's why you need explosives and big plays, because it loosens people up."

Apparently, Minshew did enough to loosen everybody up, because it was reported on Monday that he'll be the starter going forward. That can be a bumpy ride, as Minshew has proven throughout his career, but LaFleur had no choice. When the Cowboys' defense has you on pause, it's time to make a change.

"I think [Minshew] kind of showed all of us in terms of him going out there being the competitor that he is," LaFleur said on Monday after the decision was made. "Doesn't shock me. The production was pretty wild in terms of the amount of points and just moving up and down the field, but he's a competitor and he's always prepared. He's preparing like he's the starter. He's preparing like he's going to be in there for whatever snap. He supported Jacoby at a high level too. He's been a great backup for him. He's been great for the room and [I] look forward to the challenge that we have this week with him."

Nobody had any right to expect what the Tampa Bay Buccaneers did to the Atlanta Falcons last Sunday. Coming into Week 8, Jeff Ulbrich's Falcons defense was tied for third in the NFL in opponent EPA per rush at -.016, tied with the Seattle Seahawks, behind only the Jacksonville Jaguars (-0.21) and the San Francisco 49ers (-0.18).

So, how on Earth did the Bucs run 56 times for 324 yards against that estimable defense? Especially with Baker Mayfield failing to complete a single pass? The game was played in monsoon-like conditions, but it started with backs Bucky Irving and Kenneth Gainwell. In the Bucs' 16-0 win, Irving ran 27 times for 170 yards, a touchdown, seven forced missed tackles, and five runs of 10 or more yards. Gainwell ran 25 times for 140 yards, nine forced missed tackles, and five runs of 10 or more yards.

It was an ass-kicking from start to finish.

The numbers, per NFL Research and Next Gen Stats, were astonishing.

The Buccaneers became the first team in 52 seasons (Buffalo on Sept. 29, 1974) to win a game with no pass completions and the first team in 20 years (Houston on Dec. 3, 2006) to win a game with zero-or-fewer net passing yards.

Irving and Gainwell became the first pair of teammates in 50 seasons and the fourth pair of teammates in NFL history each with at least 20 rushing attempts and 125 rushing yards in the same game, joining San Francisco's Wilbur Jackson and Delvin Williams (Nov. 29, 1976), Cleveland's Pro Football Hall of Fame duo of Jim Brown and Bobby Mitchell (Dec. 13, 1959), and the Los Angeles Rams' Ron Waller and Tank Younger (Dec. 4, 1955).

Per Next Gen Stats, the Buccaneers called a designed run on 90.0% of plays against the Falcons, the highest rate in a single game since 2021 and the 2nd-highest rate in a game in the NGS era.

"I thought we did a good job until the fourth quarter," Falcons safety Jessie Bates said of his team's run-stopping efforts. "The first three quarters I really thought we contained, and I think that fourth quarter it really got out of hand. That's what happens. You have to bring your hard hat and be able to stop the run, and we didn't do that. Their defense did a good job of stopping our run, so that's what it comes down to at the end of the day."

Well, that's not quite true — Tampa Bay's rushing yards were pretty evenly distributed by quarter (68, 83, 98, and 80 yards), though the Bucs ran the ball 19 times in the final 15 minutes. Overall, they threw just about every run concept at Atlanta's defense — at least 16.1% inside zone, outside zone, man, and counter — and Atlanta, quite unusually, had no answers.

Bates had all kinds of good things to say about Irving, who has run the ball 147 times this season for 748 yards, five touchdowns, 31 forced missed tackles, and 16 runs of 10 or more yards.

"He's not your traditional back. He is almost similar to our No. 7 [Bijan Robinson] over here – you think the play is over. They run these duo runs that are supposed to hit downhill, and you've gotta cover every single gap with No. 7 [Irving]. Like I said, I thought we did a good job containing them up until a couple big runs that he had. Much respect to those guys, and we will see them again."

The rematch happens in Week 16 on Sunday, December 27. We'll see if the Buccaneers will have re-installed their passing game by then, or if they want to just go ground-and-pound until somebody (anybody) can stop them.

The Green Bay Packers have risen from 19th in Defensive DVOA in the 2025 season to fourth through Week 8 in 2026 under new defensive coordinator Jonathan Gannon, who replaced Jeff Hafley when Hafley became the Miami Dolphins' head coach. And they've done that with missing pieces on the roster. Micah Parsons has been out the entire season so far with the torn ACL he suffered last December, and linebackers Edgerrin Cooper (season-ending ruptured Achilles tendon) and Zaire Franklin (hairline shin fracture) have also been missed.

The good news for the Packers Gannon is how well the "replacements" have stepped in. And among those understudies, it's tough to imagine anybody doing better than linebacker Ty'Ron Hopper, the 2024 third-round pick from Missouri by way of Florida, who has seen his snap counts and importance to Gannon's defense spike up considerably of late.

Hopper had just 151 total snaps in 2024 and 2025 combined; he's already exceeded that by 60 this season. And he was never better than in Green Bay's 27-19 win over the Carolina Panthers last Thursday night. In that game, Hopper totaled one sack, four pressures, six solo tackles, three pass breakups, and an interception.

With that, Hopper became the first Packers player since Hall of Famer Charles Woodson in 2009 to record three pass breakups, a sack, and an interception in the same game.

"Ty'Ron Hopper. I can't say enough great things about him," Matt LaFleur said postgame. "The night he had. I think, what? Sack, PBU, interception? What a great performance. I think that's something that we did see coming more so in the pre season. I can't say enough about the job that obviously [linebackers coach] Sam Siefkes does with our inside linebackers to get them ready, and I just see a lot of growth from that group. They're playing really well."

The Packers' defense has been great already, and with Micah Parsons estimated to be back to practicing this week? It might be a whole lot better. In the meantime, guys like Hopper and recent Secret Superstar Evan Williams have been holding it down with authority.
